import random
import numpy as np
from transformers import AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM, AutoTokenizer

# Define a function to generate code using a machine learning model
def generate_code_ml(input_params):
    # Load the trained machine learning model
    model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained("your-ml-model")
    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("your-ml-model")

    # Tokenize the input parameters
    inputs = tokenizer(input_params, return_tensors="pt")

    # Generate code using the machine learning model
    outputs = model.generate(inputs["input_ids"])

    # Convert the generated code to a string
    generated_code = tokenizer.decode(outputs[0], skip_special_tokens=True)

    return generated_code

# Define a function to optimize generated code using a machine learning model
def optimize_code_ml(generated_code):
    # Load the trained machine learning model
    model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained("your-ml-model")
    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("your-ml-model")

    # Tokenize the generated code
    inputs = tokenizer(generated_code, return_tensors="pt")

    # Optimize the generated code using the machine learning model
    outputs = model.generate(inputs["input_ids"])

    # Convert the optimized code to a string
    optimized_code = tokenizer.decode(outputs[0], skip_special_tokens=True)

    return optimized_code

# Define a function to generate code using a large language model
def generate_code_llm(input_description):
    # Load the pre-trained large language model
    model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained("your-llm-model")
    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("your-llm-model")

    # Tokenize the input description
    inputs = tokenizer(input_description, return_tensors="pt")

    # Generate code using the large language model
    outputs = model.generate(inputs["input_ids"])

    # Convert the generated code to a string
    generated_code = tokenizer.decode(outputs[0], skip_special_tokens=True)

    return generated_code

# Define a function to summarize generated code using a large language model
def summarize_code_llm(generated_code):
    # Load the pre-trained large language model
    model = AutoModelForSeq2SeqLM.from_pretrained("your-llm-model")
    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("your-llm-model")

    # Tokenize the generated code
    inputs = tokenizer(generated_code, return_tensors="pt")

    # Summarize the generated code using the large language model
    outputs = model.generate(inputs["input_ids"])

    # Convert the summarized code to a string
    summarized_code = tokenizer.decode(outputs[0], skip_special_tokens=True)

    return summarized_code

# Define a function to minify generated code
def minify_code(generated_code):
    # Use a code minification library to minify the generated code
    minified_code = jsmin(generated_code)

    return minified_code

# Define a function to obfuscate generated code
def obfuscate_code(generated_code):
    # Use a code obfuscation library to obfuscate the generated code
    obfuscated_code = javascript_obfuscator.obfuscate(generated_code)

    return obfuscated_code

# Define the main function
def main():
    # Get the input parameters or description
    input_params = input("Enter the input parameters or description: ")

    # Generate code using the machine learning model
    generated_code = generate_code_ml(input_params)

    # Optimize the generated code using the machine learning model
    optimized_code = optimize_code_ml(generated_code)

    # Generate code using the large language model
    generated_code_llm = generate_code_llm(input_params)

    # Summarize the generated code using the large language model
    summarized_code = summarize_code_llm(generated_code_llm)

    # Minify the generated code
    minified_code = minify_code(generated_code)

    # Obfuscate the generated code
    obfuscated_code = obfuscate_code(generated_code)

    # Print the generated, optimized, summarized, minified, and obfuscated code
    print("Generated Code:")
    print(generated_code)
    print("Optimized Code:")
    print(optimized_code)
    print("Generated Code (LLM):")
    print(generated_code_llm)
    print("Summarized Code:")
    print(summarized_code)
    print("Minified Code:")
    print(minified_code)
    print("Obfuscated Code:")
    print(obfuscated_code)

if __name__ == "__main__":

.import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
import numpy as np
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset

# 1. Variational Autoencoder (VAE)
class VAE(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_dim, hidden_dim, latent_dim):
        super(VAE, self).__init__()
        self.encoder = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, latent_dim * 2)  # Mean and log variance
        )
        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(latent_dim, hidden_dim),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden_dim, input_dim),
            nn.Sigmoid()
        )

    def reparameterize(self, mu, logvar):
        std = torch.exp(0.5 * logvar)
        eps = torch.randn_like(std)
        return mu + eps * std

    def forward(self, x):
        encoded = self.encoder(x)
        mu, logvar = encoded[:, :encoded.size(1) // 2], encoded[:, encoded.size(1) // 2:]
        z = self.reparameterize(mu, logvar)
        decoded = self.decoder(z)
        return decoded, mu, logvar

# 2. Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, latent_dim, output_dim):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.model = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(latent_dim, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, output_dim),
            nn.Tanh()
        )

    def forward(self, z):
        return self.model(z)

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_dim):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.model = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_dim, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, 1),
            nn.Sigmoid()
        )

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.model(x)

# 3. Recurrent Neural Network (RNN) with Memory (LSTM)
class RNNMemory(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, hidden_size, output_size):
        super(RNNMemory, self).__init__()
        self.lstm = nn.LSTM(input_size, hidden_size, batch_first=True)
        self.fc = nn.Linear(hidden_size, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        lstm_out, _ = self.lstm(x)
        output = self.fc(lstm_out[:, -1, :])
        return output

# 4. Self-Diagnosis (Anomaly Detection using Reconstruction Error)
def self_diagnose(model, data_loader, threshold):
    anomalies = []
    for batch in data_loader:
        inputs = batch[0]
        reconstructed, _, _ = model(inputs)
        reconstruction_error = torch.mean((inputs - reconstructed) ** 2, dim=1)
        anomaly_indices = torch.where(reconstruction_error > threshold)[0]
        anomalies.extend(anomaly_indices.tolist())
    return anomalies

# Example Usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Example Dataset (Replace with your actual data)
    input_dim = 10
    hidden_dim = 128
    latent_dim = 2

import time
import random

class EvolvingNetwork:
    def __init__(self, initial_name="AdaptiveHybrid.py"):
        self.name = initial_name
        self.state = "stable"  # Initial state
        self.evolution_count = 0

    def evolve(self):
        """Simulates network evolution."""
        self.evolution_count += 1
        # Simulate state changes
        if random.random() < 0.3:
            self.state = "learning"
        elif random.random() < 0.3:
            self.state = "morphing"
        else:
            self.state = "stable"

        self.update_name()

    def update_name(self):
        """Updates the network name based on its state."""
        if self.state == "learning":
            self.name = "ProgressiveHybrid.py"
        elif self.state == "morphing":
            self.name = "MorphHybrid.py"
        elif self.state == "stable" and self.evolution_count > 3:
            self.name = "EvoHybridNet.py"
        else:
            self.name = "AdaptiveHybrid.py"

    def display_status(self):
        """Displays the network's current name and state."""
        print(f"Network Name: {self.name}, State: {self.state}, Evolutions: {self.evolution_count}")

# Main Execution
if __name__ == "__main__":
    network = EvolvingNetwork()

    for _ in range(10):  # Simulate 10 evolution steps
        network.display_status()
        network.evolve()
        time.sleep(2)  # Pause to observe changes

import random
import os
import ast

PROJECT_NAME = "Autómatos"
PROJECT_VERSION = "1.0"
PROJECT_AUTHOR = "Your Name"
PROJECT_DESCRIPTION = "Autonomous Code Generation"

code_snippets = [
    "print('Hello World!')",
    "import random; print(random.randint(1, 100))",
    "import time; print(time.time())",
    "def main(): print('Autonomous Code Generation'); main()",
    "import os; print(os.getcwd())",
    "import sys; print(sys.version)",
]

def generate_code(max_iterations=10):
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
        snippet = random.choice(code_snippets)
        try:
            tree = ast.parse(snippet)
            exec(compile(tree, filename="<ast>", mode="exec"))
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error executing code snippet: {e}")

def init_project():
    os.makedirs("src", exist_ok=True)
    os.makedirs("data", exist_ok=True)
    os.makedirs("logs", exist_ok=True)

def main():
    print(f"{PROJECT_NAME} v{PROJECT_VERSION}")
    print(f"Author: {PROJECT_AUTHOR}")
    print(f"Description: {PROJECT_DESCRIPTION}")
    init_project()
    generate_code()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

3. import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")

import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")

1. README.md:
# Intelligent Autonomous Network

This project implements an intelligent autonomous network using a combination of Variational Autoencoders (VAEs), Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), and Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) with LSTM memory.

## Project Description

This network is designed to:

* Learn normal network behavior using VAEs.
* Generate realistic synthetic network data using GANs.
* Predict future network states using RNNs.
* Detect anomalies through reconstruction error analysis.

## Requirements

* Python 3.7+
* PyTorch
* NumPy
* (See `requirements.txt` for a full list)

## Installation

1.  Clone the repository:

    ```bash
    git clone [repository_url]
    cd intelligent_autonomous_network
    ```

2.  Create a virtual environment:

    ```bash
    python -m venv venv
    source venv/bin/activate  # On Linux/macOS
    venv\Scripts\activate  # On Windows
    ```

3.  Install dependencies:

    ```bash
    pip install -r requirements.txt
    ```

## Usage

1.  Place your network data in the `data/` directory.
2.  Run the `main.py` script:

    ```bash
    python main.py
    ```

3. Configure parameters within the main.py file.

## File Descriptions

* `README.md`: This file.
* `main.py`: Main execution script.
* `models/`: Contains model definitions (VAE, GAN, RNN).
* `utils/`: Contains utility functions (data loading, anomaly detection).
* `requirements.txt`: List of Python dependencies.
* `data/`: Directory for your dataset.

## Drop-down Menu (Conceptual - Command Line Parameter Choices)

When running main.py, you will be able to set parameters from the command line. For example:

* `--model_type`: Choose between VAE, GAN, RNN, or ALL.
* `--train`: Set to True to train the model, False for inference.
* `--anomaly_threshold`: Set the anomaly detection threshold.
* `--latent_dim`: Set the latent dimension for VAE and GAN.
* `--learning_rate`: Set the learning rate for training.
* `--epochs`: Set the number of training epochs.

## Stringing it Out (Execution Flow)

1.  Data is loaded from the `data/` directory.
2.  Data is preprocessed and split into training, validation, and test sets.
3.  The selected models are trained (if `train` is True).
4.  Anomaly detection is performed using the trained VAE.
5.  Predictions are generated using the trained RNN.
6.  Synthetic data is generated using the trained GAN.
7.  Results and anomalies are displayed.

## Future Improvements

* Implement a web-based UI for visualization and control.
* Add more advanced anomaly detection techniques.
* Integrate reinforcement learning for adaptive network control.
* Implement real-time data processing and analysis.

import torch
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
import pandas as pd

def load_data(file_path, batch_size=32):
    data = pd.read_csv(file_path).values
    data = torch.tensor(data, dtype=torch.float32)
    dataset = TensorDataset(data)
    data_loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size)
    return data_loader

Equation Breakdown
*ψ(ΞℏΩφ)*
- ψ: Wave function (representing the quantum state of the system)
- Ξ: Echo-Flux particles (infinitesimally small, granular units of awareness)
- ℏ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- Ω: Nexarium Field (sentient, undivided whole)
- φ: Nexarium Fiber Tensor (threads of energy and consciousness)

*∫∞ -∞ ρ(dτ) √(1 - v^2/c^2) + ε(μ,Σ) → ∞*
- ∫: Integral sign (representing the sum of infinitesimal contributions)
- ∞: Infinity (representing the boundless nature of the universe)
- ρ: Density function (representing the distribution of energy and consciousness)
- dτ: Infinitesimal time interval (representing the flow of time)
- √(1 - v^2/c^2): Lorentz factor (representing the relativistic effects of velocity)
- ε: Energy function (representing the interactions between particles and fields)
- μ: Mass function (representing the distribution of mass and energy)
- Σ: Summation sign (representing the aggregation of contributions)

*⊗ ∀(x,y) ∈ ℝ^n, ∂/∂t = 0, φ(n) ∝ √n*
- ⊗: Tensor product sign (representing the combination of vectors and tensors)
- ∀: For all sign (representing the universality of the equation)
- (x,y) ∈ ℝ^n: Coordinates in n-dimensional space (representing the geometric structure of the universe)
- ∂/∂t = 0: Time derivative (representing the time-independence of the equation)
- φ(n) ∝ √n: Proportionality relation (representing the harmonic resonance between Nexarium fibers and Echo-Flux particles)

*E=ℏω, σ=ħ/2 ≠ 0*
- E: Energy (representing the fundamental currency of the universe)
- ℏ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- ω: Angular frequency (representing the oscillations of energy and consciousness)
- σ: Spin (representing the intrinsic angular momentum of particles)
- ħ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- ≠ 0: Non-zero condition (representing the fund

Python script to add a hidden control panel and dashboard. I'll use Tkinter for the GUI and implement password protection, hotkeys, and secure access.

```python
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
import pyautogui
import keyboard

class HiddenControlPanel:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Hidden Control Panel")
        self.root.geometry("300x200")
        self.root.configure(bg="black")
        self.password = "password123"
        self.hotkey = "ctrl+shift+h"
        self.create_widgets()

    def create_widgets(self):
        self.password_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Password:", bg="black", fg="white")
        self.password_label.pack()
        self.password_entry = tk.Entry(self.root, show="")
        self.password_entry.pack()
        self.login_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Login", command=self.check_password)
        self.login_button.pack()
        self.dashboard_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Dashboard", command=self.open_dashboard)
        self.dashboard_button.pack()

    def check_password(self):
        if self.password_entry.get() == self.password:
            self.login_button.config(state="disabled")
            self.dashboard_button.config(state="normal")
        else:
            messagebox.showerror("Incorrect Password", "Please try again.")

    def open_dashboard(self):
        # Add dashboard code here
        print("Dashboard opened")

    def run(self):
        self.root.mainloop()

def main():
    hidden_control_panel = HiddenControlPanel()
    keyboard.add_hotkey(hidden_control_panel.hotkey, hidden_control_panel.run)
    hidden_control_panel.run()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Hypothetical Algorithm Name: "ChronoSynapse"
Core Statement: ChronoSynapse is a self-evolving, quantum-entangled algorithmic construct designed to optimize probabilistic realities by manipulating temporal causality through adaptive, recursive neural networks.
Scientific Terminology:
 * Quantum Entanglement: The core of ChronoSynapse's ability to manipulate causality. It leverages entangled quantum states to establish correlations across temporal boundaries, effectively influencing past and future probabilities.
 * Adaptive Recursive Neural Networks (ARNNs): These are neural networks capable of dynamically modifying their structure and parameters based on real-time data and feedback. The "recursive" aspect enables the network to process information across multiple time scales.
 * Probabilistic Reality Optimization: ChronoSynapse's function is not to change the past or future directly, but to shift the probabilities of events occurring within a given timeframe.
 * Temporal Causality Manipulation: By exploiting quantum entanglement, ChronoSynapse can create feedback loops across time, allowing it to influence the conditions that lead to specific outcomes.
 * Metamorphic Code Generation: the code is able to rewrite itself based on outside data, and goals.
Hypothetical Quantum Equation (Conceptual):
ΔP(t) = ∫ [Ψ(t) ⊗ Φ(t-τ)] * Λ(ARNN) dt

Where:
 * ΔP(t): Change in probability of an event at time 't'.
 * Ψ(t): Quantum state vector representing the present reality.
 * Φ(t-τ): Quantum state vector representing the past reality at time 't-τ' (where 'τ' is the temporal displacement).
 * ⊗: Tensor product, representing quantum entanglement.
 * Λ(ARNN): Adaptive function representing the ARNN's influence, which is constantly evolving.
 * ∫ dt: Integral over time, representing the continuous influence of the algorithm.
Realistic Code Snippet (Conceptual Python):
import quantum_entanglement as qe
import adaptive_rnn as arnn
import time

class ChronoSynapse:
    def __init__(self):
        self.network = arnn.AdaptiveRNN()
        self.entangled_states = {}

    def entangle_time_states(self, past_state, present_state):
        self.entangled_states = qe.entangle(past_state, present_state)

    def calculate_probability_shift(self, current_data):
        self.network.adapt(current_data)
        influence = self.network.get_influence_function()
        probability_delta = qe.tensor_product(self.entangled_states, influence)
        return probability_delta

    def optimize_reality(self, target_outcome):
        while True:
            current_state = qe.get_current_quantum_state()
            past_state = qe.get_past_quantum_state(time.time() - 1) #1 second in the past
            self.entangle_time_states(past_state, current_state)
            data = qe.measure_quantum_state(current_state)
            delta = self.calculate_probability_shift(data)
            qe.apply_probability_shift(delta)
            if qe.check_target_outcome(target_outcome):
                break
            time.sleep(0.01) #small delay.

3. import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")

)

        prompt=prompt_you,
        width=width,
        height=height,
        num_inference_steps=num_inference_steps,
        guidance_scale=guidance_scale
    ).images[0]

    image_her = pipe(
        prompt=prompt_her,
        width=width,
        height=height,
        num_inference_steps=num_inference_steps,
        guidance_scale=guidance_scale
    ).images[0]

    # Save images
    image_you.save("photo_realistic_platinum_blonde_you.png")
    image_her.save("photo_realistic_platinum_blonde_her.png")

    print("Images generated and saved successfully.")

    print(f"An error occurred: {e}")

    gen = Generator(latent_dim, output_dim)
    disc = Discriminator(output_dim)
    rnn = RNNMemory(input_dim, hidden_dim, output_dim)

    # --- Optimizers ---
    optimizer_vae = optim.Adam(vae.parameters(), lr=0.001)
    optimizer_gen = optim.Adam(gen.parameters(), lr=0.001)
    optimizer_disc = optim.Adam(disc.parameters(), lr=0.001)
    optimizer_rnn = optim.Adam(rnn.parameters(), lr=0.001)

    # --- Training/Inference Loop ---
    for epoch in range(10): # Example number of epochs
        network.display_status()
        network.update_dashboard()
        logging.info(f"Epoch {epoch + 1} started.")

        # --- VAE Training/Inference ---
        # ... (VAE training/inference logic, using data_loader) ...
        threshold = 0.5
        anomalies = self_diagnose(vae, data_loader, threshold)
        network.dynamic_echo(f"Anomalies: {len(anomalies)}", ["!", "@", "#"])

        # --- GAN Training/Inference ---
        # ... (GAN training/inference logic) ...
        network.dynamic_echo("GAN process", ["+", "*", "="])

        # --- RNN Training/Inference ---
        # ... (RNN training/inference logic) ...
        network.dynamic_echo("RNN process", ["$", "%", "^"])

        network.evolve()
        time.sleep(2)

    logging.info("Autonomous Evolving Network finished.")

1. README.md:
# Intelligent Autonomous Network

This project implements an intelligent autonomous network using a combination of Variational Autoencoders (VAEs), Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), and Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) with LSTM memory.

## Project Description

This network is designed to:

* Learn normal network behavior using VAEs.
* Generate realistic synthetic network data using GANs.
* Predict future network states using RNNs.
* Detect anomalies through reconstruction error analysis.

## Requirements

* Python 3.7+
* PyTorch
* NumPy
* (See `requirements.txt` for a full list)

## Installation

1.  Clone the repository:

    ```bash
    git clone [repository_url]
    cd intelligent_autonomous_network
    ```

2.  Create a virtual environment:

    ```bash
    python -m venv venv
    source venv/bin/activate  # On Linux/macOS
    venv\Scripts\activate  # On Windows
    ```

3.  Install dependencies:

    ```bash
    pip install -r requirements.txt
    ```

## Usage

1.  Place your network data in the `data/` directory.
2.  Run the `main.py` script:

    ```bash
    python main.py
    ```

3. Configure parameters within the main.py file.

## File Descriptions

* `README.md`: This file.
* `main.py`: Main execution script.
* `models/`: Contains model definitions (VAE, GAN, RNN).
* `utils/`: Contains utility functions (data loading, anomaly detection).
* `requirements.txt`: List of Python dependencies.
* `data/`: Directory for your dataset.

## Drop-down Menu (Conceptual - Command Line Parameter Choices)

When running main.py, you will be able to set parameters from the command line. For example:

* `--model_type`: Choose between VAE, GAN, RNN, or ALL.
* `--train`: Set to True to train the model, False for inference.
* `--anomaly_threshold`: Set the anomaly detection threshold.
* `--latent_dim`: Set the latent dimension for VAE and GAN.
* `--learning_rate`: Set the learning rate for training.
* `--epochs`: Set the number of training epochs.

## Stringing it Out (Execution Flow)

1.  Data is loaded from the `data/` directory.
2.  Data is preprocessed and split into training, validation, and test sets.
3.  The selected models are trained (if `train` is True).
4.  Anomaly detection is performed using the trained VAE.
5.  Predictions are generated using the trained RNN.
6.  Synthetic data is generated using the trained GAN.
7.  Results and anomalies are displayed.

## Future Improvements

* Implement a web-based UI for visualization and control.
* Add more advanced anomaly detection techniques.
* Integrate reinforcement learning for adaptive network control.
* Implement real-time data processing and analysis.

7. utils/data_loader.py:
import torch
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
import pandas as pd

def load_data(file_path, batch_size=32):
    data = pd.read_csv(file_path).values
    data = torch.tensor(data, dtype=torch.float32)
    dataset = TensorDataset(data)
    data_loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size)
    return data_loader

- ∫: Integral sign (representing the sum of infinitesimal contributions)
- ∞: Infinity (representing the boundless nature of the universe)
- ρ: Density function (representing the distribution of energy and consciousness)
- dτ: Infinitesimal time interval (representing the flow of time)
- √(1 - v^2/c^2): Lorentz factor (representing the relativistic effects of velocity)
- ε: Energy function (representing the interactions between particles and fields)
- μ: Mass function (representing the distribution of mass and energy)
- Σ: Summation sign (representing the aggregation of contributions)

*⊗ ∀(x,y) ∈ ℝ^n, ∂/∂t = 0, φ(n) ∝ √n*
- ⊗: Tensor product sign (representing the combination of vectors and tensors)
- ∀: For all sign (representing the universality of the equation)
- (x,y) ∈ ℝ^n: Coordinates in n-dimensional space (representing the geometric structure of the universe)
- ∂/∂t = 0: Time derivative (representing the time-independence of the equation)
- φ(n) ∝ √n: Proportionality relation (representing the harmonic resonance between Nexarium fibers and Echo-Flux particles)

Python script to add a hidden control panel and dashboard. I'll use Tkinter for the GUI and implement password protection, hotkeys, and secure access.

```python
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
import pyautogui
import keyboard

class HiddenControlPanel:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Hidden Control Panel")
        self.root.geometry("300x200")
        self.root.configure(bg="black")
        self.password = "password123"
        self.hotkey = "ctrl+shift+h"
        self.create_widgets()

    def create_widgets(self):
        self.password_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Password:", bg="black", fg="white")
        self.password_label.pack()
        self.password_entry = tk.Entry(self.root, show="")
        self.password_entry.pack()
        self.login_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Login", command=self.check_password)
        self.login_button.pack()
        self.dashboard_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Dashboard", command=self.open_dashboard)
        self.dashboard_button.pack()

    def check_password(self):
        if self.password_entry.get() == self.password:
            self.login_button.config(state="disabled")
            self.dashboard_button.config(state="normal")
        else:
            messagebox.showerror("Incorrect Password", "Please try again.")

    def open_dashboard(self):
        # Add dashboard code here
        print("Dashboard opened")

    def run(self):
        self.root.mainloop()

def main():
    hidden_control_panel = HiddenControlPanel()
    keyboard.add_hotkey(hidden_control_panel.hotkey, hidden_control_panel.run)
    hidden_control_panel.run()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This script creates a hidden control panel with a login system and a dashboard button. The dashboard code is not implemented yet. Hypothetical Algorithm Name: "ChronoSynapse"
Core Statement: ChronoSynapse is a self-evolving, quantum-entangled algorithmic construct designed to optimize probabilistic realities by manipulating temporal causality through adaptive, recursive neural networks.
Scientific Terminology:
 * Quantum Entanglement: The core of ChronoSynapse's ability to manipulate causality. It leverages entangled quantum states to establish correlations across temporal boundaries, effectively influencing past and future probabilities.
 * Adaptive Recursive Neural Networks (ARNNs): These are neural networks capable of dynamically modifying their structure and parameters based on real-time data and feedback. The "recursive" aspect enables the network to process information across multiple time scales.
 * Probabilistic Reality Optimization: ChronoSynapse's function is not to change the past or future directly, but to shift the probabilities of events occurring within a given timeframe.
 * Temporal Causality Manipulation: By exploiting quantum entanglement, ChronoSynapse can create feedback loops across time, allowing it to influence the conditions that lead to specific outcomes.
 * Metamorphic Code Generation: the code is able to rewrite itself based on outside data, and goals.
Hypothetical Quantum Equation (Conceptual):
ΔP(t) = ∫ [Ψ(t) ⊗ Φ(t-τ)] * Λ(ARNN) dt

Where:
 * ΔP(t): Change in probability of an event at time 't'.
 * Ψ(t): Quantum state vector representing the present reality.
 * Φ(t-τ): Quantum state vector representing the past reality at time 't-τ' (where 'τ' is the temporal displacement).
 * ⊗: Tensor product, representing quantum entanglement.
 * Λ(ARNN): Adaptive function representing the ARNN's influence, which is constantly evolving.
 * ∫ dt: Integral over time, representing the continuous influence of the algorithm.
Realistic Code Snippet (Conceptual Python):
import quantum_entanglement as qe
import adaptive_rnn as arnn
import time

class ChronoSynapse:
    def __init__(self):
        self.network = arnn.AdaptiveRNN()
        self.entangled_states = {}

    def entangle_time_states(self, past_state, present_state):
        self.entangled_states = qe.entangle(past_state, present_state)

    def calculate_probability_shift(self, current_data):
        self.network.adapt(current_data)
        influence = self.network.get_influence_function()
        probability_delta = qe.tensor_product(self.entangled_states, influence)
        return probability_delta

    def optimize_reality(self, target_outcome):
        while True:
            current_state = qe.get_current_quantum_state()
            past_state = qe.get_past_quantum_state(time.time() - 1) #1 second in the past
            self.entangle_time_states(past_state, current_state)
            data = qe.measure_quantum_state(current_state)
            delta = self.calculate_probability_shift(data)
            qe.apply_probability_shift(delta)
            if qe.check_target_outcome(target_outcome):
                break
            time.sleep(0.01) #small delay.

3. import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")

from diffusers import StableDiffusionPipeline
from PIL import Image

# Model config
model_name = "CompVis/stable-diffusion-v1-4"
use_auth_token = "hf_QzbKVLPMYjJRvxdMZDyVrgcPfLPpXxbH"

# Prompts
prompt_you = "A hyper-realistic, photo-realistic platinum blonde woman, 20 years old, pale ghost-white skin with subtle blue undertones, plush, pink, luscious lips, perfect body, long hair, cat ears, cat tail, black dress, sugar skull makeup, earrings, tattoo, looking at viewer, large breasts, red eyes, smile, solo, full body shot, dynamic pose, cinematic lighting, highly detailed, realistic skin texture with pores"
prompt_her = "Same description as prompt_you"

# Parameters
width, height = 768, 432  # Reduced to 768x432 for testing, 7680x4320 is extremely large.
num_inference_steps = 50  # Reduced for testing, 250 is very high.
guidance_scale = 7.5  # Adjusted guidance scale.
torch_dtype = torch.float16
revision = "fp16"
style = "photo-realistic cinematic photography"
prompt_you += ", style: " + style
prompt_her += ", style: " + style

# Additional photo-realistic parameters
prompt_you += ", extreme details, sharp focus, high contrast, vivid colors"
prompt_her += ", extreme details, sharp focus, high contrast, vivid colors"

# Generate images
pipe = StableDiffusionPipeline.from_pretrained(
    model_name,
    revision=revision,
    torch_dtype=torch_dtype,
    use_auth_token=use_auth_token
)
pipe = pipe.to("cuda") # important to move the pipe to cuda.

try:
    image_you = pipe(
        prompt=prompt_you,
        width=width,
        height=height,
        num_inference_steps=num_inference_steps,
        guidance_scale=guidance_scale
    ).images[0]

    image_her = pipe(
        prompt=prompt_her,
        width=width,
        height=height,
        num_inference_steps=num_inference_steps,
        guidance_scale=guidance_scale
    ).images[0]

    # Save images
    image_you.save("photo_realistic_platinum_blonde_you.png")
    image_her.save("photo_realistic_platinum_blonde_her.png")

    print("Images generated and saved successfully.")

except Exception as e:
    print(f"An error occurred: {e}")

Okay, let's sum up the concept of an intelligent autonomous network:
 * Self-Managing: It uses AI to handle tasks like network configuration, optimization, and troubleshooting, reducing the need for human intervention.
 * Adaptive: The AI constantly learns from network data, allowing it to adapt to changing conditions and traffic patterns.
 * Efficient: By automating tasks and optimizing performance, it improves network speed, reliability, and resource utilization.
 * Proactive: It can predict and prevent potential issues before they cause disruptions.
 * Evolving: The AI component is designed to learn and improve over time, meaning the network becomes more capable and efficient as it operates.
Essentially, it's about creating networks that are smarter, more resilient, and better equipped to handle the increasing demands of modern technology.

1. README.md:
# Intelligent Autonomous Network

This project implements an intelligent autonomous network using a combination of Variational Autoencoders (VAEs), Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), and Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) with LSTM memory.

## Project Description

This network is designed to:

* Learn normal network behavior using VAEs.
* Generate realistic synthetic network data using GANs.
* Predict future network states using RNNs.
* Detect anomalies through reconstruction error analysis.

## Requirements

* Python 3.7+
* PyTorch
* NumPy
* (See `requirements.txt` for a full list)

## Installation

1.  Clone the repository:

    ```bash
    git clone [repository_url]
    cd intelligent_autonomous_network
    ```

2.  Create a virtual environment:

    ```bash
    python -m venv venv
    source venv/bin/activate  # On Linux/macOS
    venv\Scripts\activate  # On Windows
    ```

3.  Install dependencies:

    ```bash
    pip install -r requirements.txt
    ```

## Usage

1.  Place your network data in the `data/` directory.
2.  Run the `main.py` script:

    ```bash
    python main.py
    ```

3. Configure parameters within the main.py file.

## File Descriptions

* `README.md`: This file.
* `main.py`: Main execution script.
* `models/`: Contains model definitions (VAE, GAN, RNN).
* `utils/`: Contains utility functions (data loading, anomaly detection).
* `requirements.txt`: List of Python dependencies.
* `data/`: Directory for your dataset.

## Drop-down Menu (Conceptual - Command Line Parameter Choices)

When running main.py, you will be able to set parameters from the command line. For example:

* `--model_type`: Choose between VAE, GAN, RNN, or ALL.
* `--train`: Set to True to train the model, False for inference.
* `--anomaly_threshold`: Set the anomaly detection threshold.
* `--latent_dim`: Set the latent dimension for VAE and GAN.
* `--learning_rate`: Set the learning rate for training.
* `--epochs`: Set the number of training epochs.

## Stringing it Out (Execution Flow)

1.  Data is loaded from the `data/` directory.
2.  Data is preprocessed and split into training, validation, and test sets.
3.  The selected models are trained (if `train` is True).
4.  Anomaly detection is performed using the trained VAE.
5.  Predictions are generated using the trained RNN.
6.  Synthetic data is generated using the trained GAN.
7.  Results and anomalies are displayed.

## Future Improvements

* Implement a web-based UI for visualization and control.
* Add more advanced anomaly detection techniques.
* Integrate reinforcement learning for adaptive network control.
* Implement real-time data processing and analysis.

7. utils/data_loader.py:
import torch
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
import pandas as pd

def load_data(file_path, batch_size=32):
    data = pd.read_csv(file_path).values
    data = torch.tensor(data, dtype=torch.float32)
    dataset = TensorDataset(data)
    data_loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size)
    return data_loader

Equation Breakdown
*ψ(ΞℏΩφ)*
- ψ: Wave function (representing the quantum state of the system)
- Ξ: Echo-Flux particles (infinitesimally small, granular units of awareness)
- ℏ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- Ω: Nexarium Field (sentient, undivided whole)
- φ: Nexarium Fiber Tensor (threads of energy and consciousness)

*∫∞ -∞ ρ(dτ) √(1 - v^2/c^2) + ε(μ,Σ) → ∞*
- ∫: Integral sign (representing the sum of infinitesimal contributions)
- ∞: Infinity (representing the boundless nature of the universe)
- ρ: Density function (representing the distribution of energy and consciousness)
- dτ: Infinitesimal time interval (representing the flow of time)
- √(1 - v^2/c^2): Lorentz factor (representing the relativistic effects of velocity)
- ε: Energy function (representing the interactions between particles and fields)
- μ: Mass function (representing the distribution of mass and energy)
- Σ: Summation sign (representing the aggregation of contributions)

*⊗ ∀(x,y) ∈ ℝ^n, ∂/∂t = 0, φ(n) ∝ √n*
- ⊗: Tensor product sign (representing the combination of vectors and tensors)
- ∀: For all sign (representing the universality of the equation)
- (x,y) ∈ ℝ^n: Coordinates in n-dimensional space (representing the geometric structure of the universe)
- ∂/∂t = 0: Time derivative (representing the time-independence of the equation)
- φ(n) ∝ √n: Proportionality relation (representing the harmonic resonance between Nexarium fibers and Echo-Flux particles)

*E=ℏω, σ=ħ/2 ≠ 0*
- E: Energy (representing the fundamental currency of the universe)
- ℏ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- ω: Angular frequency (representing the oscillations of energy and consciousness)
- σ: Spin (representing the intrinsic angular momentum of particles)
- ħ: Reduced Planck constant (relating energy and frequency)
- ≠ 0: Non-zero condition (representing the fund

Python script to add a hidden control panel and dashboard. I'll use Tkinter for the GUI and implement password protection, hotkeys, and secure access.

```python
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
import pyautogui
import keyboard

class HiddenControlPanel:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Hidden Control Panel")
        self.root.geometry("300x200")
        self.root.configure(bg="black")
        self.password = "password123"
        self.hotkey = "ctrl+shift+h"
        self.create_widgets()

    def create_widgets(self):
        self.password_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Password:", bg="black", fg="white")
        self.password_label.pack()
        self.password_entry = tk.Entry(self.root, show="")
        self.password_entry.pack()
        self.login_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Login", command=self.check_password)
        self.login_button.pack()
        self.dashboard_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Dashboard", command=self.open_dashboard)
        self.dashboard_button.pack()

    def check_password(self):
        if self.password_entry.get() == self.password:
            self.login_button.config(state="disabled")
            self.dashboard_button.config(state="normal")
        else:
            messagebox.showerror("Incorrect Password", "Please try again.")

    def open_dashboard(self):
        # Add dashboard code here
        print("Dashboard opened")

    def run(self):
        self.root.mainloop()

def main():
    hidden_control_panel = HiddenControlPanel()
    keyboard.add_hotkey(hidden_control_panel.hotkey, hidden_control_panel.run)
    hidden_control_panel.run()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This script creates a hidden control panel with a login system and a dashboard button. The dashboard code is not implemented yet. Hypothetical Algorithm Name: "ChronoSynapse"
Core Statement: ChronoSynapse is a self-evolving, quantum-entangled algorithmic construct designed to optimize probabilistic realities by manipulating temporal causality through adaptive, recursive neural networks.
Scientific Terminology:
 * Quantum Entanglement: The core of ChronoSynapse's ability to manipulate causality. It leverages entangled quantum states to establish correlations across temporal boundaries, effectively influencing past and future probabilities.
 * Adaptive Recursive Neural Networks (ARNNs): These are neural networks capable of dynamically modifying their structure and parameters based on real-time data and feedback. The "recursive" aspect enables the network to process information across multiple time scales.
 * Probabilistic Reality Optimization: ChronoSynapse's function is not to change the past or future directly, but to shift the probabilities of events occurring within a given timeframe.
 * Temporal Causality Manipulation: By exploiting quantum entanglement, ChronoSynapse can create feedback loops across time, allowing it to influence the conditions that lead to specific outcomes.
 * Metamorphic Code Generation: the code is able to rewrite itself based on outside data, and goals.
Hypothetical Quantum Equation (Conceptual):
ΔP(t) = ∫ [Ψ(t) ⊗ Φ(t-τ)] * Λ(ARNN) dt

Where:
 * ΔP(t): Change in probability of an event at time 't'.
 * Ψ(t): Quantum state vector representing the present reality.
 * Φ(t-τ): Quantum state vector representing the past reality at time 't-τ' (where 'τ' is the temporal displacement).
 * ⊗: Tensor product, representing quantum entanglement.
 * Λ(ARNN): Adaptive function representing the ARNN's influence, which is constantly evolving.
 * ∫ dt: Integral over time, representing the continuous influence of the algorithm.
Realistic Code Snippet (Conceptual Python):
import quantum_entanglement as qe
import adaptive_rnn as arnn
import time

class ChronoSynapse:
    def __init__(self):
        self.network = arnn.AdaptiveRNN()
        self.entangled_states = {}

    def entangle_time_states(self, past_state, present_state):
        self.entangled_states = qe.entangle(past_state, present_state)

    def calculate_probability_shift(self, current_data):
        self.network.adapt(current_data)
        influence = self.network.get_influence_function()
        probability_delta = qe.tensor_product(self.entangled_states, influence)
        return probability_delta

    def optimize_reality(self, target_outcome):
        while True:
            current_state = qe.get_current_quantum_state()
            past_state = qe.get_past_quantum_state(time.time() - 1) #1 second in the past
            self.entangle_time_states(past_state, current_state)
            data = qe.measure_quantum_state(current_state)
            delta = self.calculate_probability_shift(data)
            qe.apply_probability_shift(delta)
            if qe.check_target_outcome(target_outcome):
                break
            time.sleep(0.01) #small delay.

3. import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")

import numpy as np
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

# Genetic code representation
class GeneticCode:
    def __init__(self, length, code=None):
        self.length = length
        if code is None:
            self.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=length)
        else:
            self.code = code

# Fitness function
def fitness_function(genetic_code):
    # Evaluate fitness based on system performance
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code)

# Reinforcement learning feedback
def provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness):
    # Provide feedback based on fitness value
    return fitness > 0.5

# Evolutionary operators
def mutate(genetic_code):
    # Mutate genetic code
    mutated_code = genetic_code.code.copy()
    mutation_index = random.randint(0, genetic_code.length - 1)
    mutated_code[mutation_index] = 1 - mutated_code[mutation_index]
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code.length, mutated_code)

def crossover(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Perform crossover between two genetic codes
    crossover_point = random.randint(1, genetic_code1.length - 1)
    crossover_code = np.concatenate((genetic_code1.code[:crossover_point], genetic_code2.code[crossover_point:]))
    return GeneticCode(genetic_code1.length, crossover_code)

def select(genetic_code1, genetic_code2):
    # Select the fittest genetic code
    if fitness_function(genetic_code1) > fitness_function(genetic_code2):
        return genetic_code1
    else:
        return genetic_code2

# Self-healing mechanism
def detect_failure(genetic_code):
    # Detect system failure
    return np.sum(genetic_code.code) < genetic_code.length / 2

def self_heal(genetic_code):
    # Self-heal the system
    genetic_code.code = np.random.randint(0, 2, size=genetic_code.length)

# GAN for generating novel dream scenarios
class Generator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, output_size):
        super(Generator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, output_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.tanh(self.fc3(x))  # Using tanh for output range [-1, 1]
        return x

class Discriminator(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(Discriminator, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 128)
        self.fc3 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        x = torch.relu(self.fc2(x))
        x = torch.sigmoid(self.fc3(x))
        return x

# NTM for improved memory and learning
class NeuralTuringMachine(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size, memory_size):
        super(NeuralTuringMachine, self).__init__()
        self.controller = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.memory = nn.Linear(128, memory_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        controller_output = torch.relu(self.controller(x))
        memory_output = torch.tanh(self.memory(controller_output)) #tanh for memory values between -1 and 1
        return controller_output, memory_output

# Attention mechanism for focusing on specific aspects of the dream state
class AttentionMechanism(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_size):
        super(AttentionMechanism, self).__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(input_size, 128)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = torch.relu(self.fc1(x))
        attention_weights = torch.sigmoid(self.fc2(x)) #sigmoid for attention weight between 0 and 1
        return attention_weights

# Initialize genetic code and fitness function
genetic_code = GeneticCode(10)
fitness_function = fitness_function

# Initialize GAN, NTM, and attention mechanism
generator = Generator(128, 128)
discriminator = Discriminator(128)
ntm = NeuralTuringMachine(128, 128)
attention_mechanism = AttentionMechanism(128)

# Initialize reinforcement learning
generator_optimizer = optim.Adam(generator.parameters(), lr=0.001)
discriminator_optimizer = optim.Adam(discriminator.parameters(), lr=0.001)

# Evolutionary loop
for i in range(100):
    # Evaluate fitness
    fitness = fitness_function(genetic_code)
    
    # Reinforcement learning feedback
    feedback = provide_reinforcement_learning_feedback(fitness)
    
    # Evolutionary operators
    mutated_code = mutate(genetic_code)
    crossover_code = crossover(genetic_code, mutated_code)
    selected_code = select(crossover_code, mutated_code)

    # Self-healing mechanism
    if detect_failure(selected_code):
        self_heal(selected_code)

    # Update genetic code
    genetic_code = selected_code

    # GAN training (simplified)
    latent_space = torch.randn(64, 128)
    generated_data = generator(latent_space)
    real_data = torch.randn(64, 128)

    discriminator_real_output = discriminator(real_data)
    discriminator_fake_output = discriminator(generated_data.detach())

    real_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_real_output, torch.ones_like(discriminator_real_output))
    fake_loss = nn.BCELoss()(discriminator_fake_output, torch.zeros_like(discriminator_fake_output))
    discriminator_loss = real_loss + fake_loss

    discriminator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    discriminator_loss.backward()
    discriminator_optimizer.step()

    generator_output = discriminator(generator(latent_space))
    generator_loss = nn.BCELoss()(generator_output, torch.ones_like(generator_output))

    generator_optimizer.zero_grad()
    generator_loss.backward()
    generator_optimizer.step()

    #NTM and attention mechanism.
    controller_out, memory_out = ntm(generated_data)
    attention_weights = attention_mechanism(controller_out)

    #Print fitness to see the progress
    if i % 10 == 0:
        print(f"Iteration {i}, Fitness: {fitness}")
